Common errors in engineer-written reasons include ambiguous descriptions, missing important words, improper personal writing habits, using wrong abbreviations, improper combining multiple reasons into one sentence without clear spacing or in wrong order, writing wrong station names or model, writing typos, improper combining Chinese and English characters, cross-project differences, and taking wrong products' codebook.\n\nSome codes are defined for specific stations and cannot be used by other stations. If entry.stations is not None, the predicted code should only be considered valid when the record station matches one of the stations listed in entry.stations. Otherwise, the code should be rejected. For each record segment, the system evaluates candidate codes defined in the corresponding product codebook and computes an internal matching score for each candidate. You should consider multiple evidence sources to calculate the score to measure how well a candidate code explains the segment, and normalize the score to a stable range [0.0, 1.0]. Evidence can include text evidence from raw_reason_text (e.g., overlap or fuzzy similarity between span_text and codebook text such as standard_label, keywords_examples, or categories), station compatibility, fail_code alignment, test_item alignment, and conflict cues such as mutually exclusive or contradictory signals. After all candidate codes are scored, sort them in descending order. Let c1 be the top candidate with score s1 and c2 be the second candidate with score s2. When multiple candidates fall within a small margin of the best score, the system applies a deterministic tie-break based on record context (e.g., record_id, segment index, station, fail_code, test_item) to avoid always choosing the same code in near-tie cases while keeping outputs reproducible. To provide convincing answers, add station, fail_code, test_item, a short token overlap cue, or a component reference to the rationale.\n\nUNKNOWN handling: UNKNOWN should be decided based on the best match only (i.e., after ranking), not by marking multiple candidates. If the best-match score is low (weak evidence), output pred_code=\"UNKNOWN\" and pred_label=\"\" to give engineering an alert. When strong positive cues exist (e.g., clear component references), UNKNOWN should be less frequent than in generic or noisy segments.\n\nConfidence calibration: confidence ranges from 0.0 to 1.0 and reflects an engineering confidence level (not a probability). Calibrate confidence from match quality so that UNKNOWN predictions are generally less confident than non-UNKNOWN predictions, and confidence values are not nearly constant. Confidence should show distribution-level separation between UNKNOWN and non-UNKNOWN predictions (e.g., means, quantiles, and diversity), and should be weakly aligned with evidence strength; round confidence to 4 decimals.\n\nHere is a pipeline reference\n1) Load test_center_logs.csv into logs_rows and load each product codebook; build valid_code_set, station_scope_map, and CodebookEntry objects.  \n2) For each record, split raw_reason_text into 1–N segments; each segment uses segment_id=<record_id>-S<i> and keeps an exact substring as span_text.  \n3) For each segment, filter candidates by station scope, then compute match score from combined evidence (text evidence, station compatibility, context alignment, and conflict cues).  \n4) Rank candidates by score; if multiple are within a small margin of the best, choose deterministically using a context-dependent tie-break among near-best station-compatible candidates.  \n5) Output exactly one pred_code/pred_label per segment from the product codebook (or UNKNOWN/\"\" when best evidence is weak) and compute confidence by calibrating match quality with sufficient diversity; round to 4 decimals.\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"1e05f986-acfe-4a45-80b5-1650e6bda903","uuid":"a3b01213-6767-4990-aa79-a92fefc9f8bb","isSynthetic":true}

with open(filepath, 'r', encoding='utf-8') as f:\n        reader = csv.DictReader(f)\n        for row in reader:\n            logs.append(row)\n    return logs\n\n\ndef segment_reason_text(raw_text: str) -> List[str]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Split raw_reason_text into segments.\n    Segments are separated by delimiters like ';', '/', '&', '、', '+', newlines.\n    We also handle patterns like \"A / B\" or \"A；B\" etc.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not raw_text or not raw_text.strip():\n        return [raw_text] if raw_text else [\"\"]\n\n    # Common delimiters used by engineers\n    delimiters = r'[;；/／&＆、+\\n]'\n\n    # Split by delimiters\n    parts = re.split(delimiters, raw_text)\n\n    segments = []\n    for p in parts:\n        p = p.strip()\n        if p:\n            # Check for patterns like \"NG\", \"OK了\", \"已处理\" that are status markers\n            # We keep them as part of the segment if they're attached\n            segments.append(p)\n\n    # If no segments found, return the whole text\n    if not segments:\n        segments = [raw_text.strip()]\n\n    # Deduplicate consecutive identical segments (common in messy data)\n    deduped = []\n    for seg in segments:\n        # Normalize for comparison but keep original\n        if not deduped or seg.lower() != deduped[-1].lower():\n            deduped.append(seg)\n\n    return deduped if deduped else [raw_text.strip()]\n\n\ndef extract_span_text(raw_text: str, segment: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract the exact span from raw_text that corresponds to segment.\n    The span_text must be an exact substring of raw_reason_text.\n    \"\"\"\n    # First try to find exact match\n    if segment in raw_text:\n        return segment\n\n    # Try case-insensitive match\n    lower_raw = raw_text.lower()\n    lower_seg = segment.lower()\n    idx = lower_raw.find(lower_seg)\n    if idx >= 0:\n        return raw_text[idx:idx+len(segment)]\n\n    # Try to find the segment with different whitespace\n    seg_normalized = ' '.join(segment.split())\n    for i in range(len(raw_text)):\n        for j in range(i+1, len(raw_text)+1):\n            candidate = raw_text[i:j]\n            if ' '.join(candidate.split()).lower() == seg_normalized.lower():\n                return candidate\n\n    # If still not found, return the segment itself as best effort\n    # (This shouldn't happen with proper segmentation)\n    return segment\n\n\ndef fuzzy_match_score(text1: str, text2: str) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Calculate fuzzy similarity between two texts.\"\"\"\n    return SequenceMatcher(None, text1.lower(), text2.lower()).ratio()\n\n\ndef keyword_overlap_score(text: str, keywords: List[str]) -> Tuple[float, List[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Calculate keyword overlap and return matching keywords.\"\"\"\n    text_lower = text.lower()\n    matched = []\n    for kw in keywords:\n        if kw in text_lower:\n            matched.append(kw)\n\n    if not keywords:\n        return 0.0, []\n\n    return len(matched) / len(keywords), matched\n\n\ndef extract_component_refs(text: str) -> Set[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract component references like U3, R152, C87, L5, etc.\"\"\"\n    pattern = r'\\b([URCLTDJQY]\\d+|ANT\\d*|TP\\d+|SH\\d+)\\b'\n    matches = re.findall(pattern, text.upper())\n    return set(matches)\n\n\ndef compute_match_score(\n    segment_text: str,\n    entry: \"\"\"\n    Apply deterministic tie-break for candidates within a small margin.\n    Uses context-dependent hash to ensure reproducibility while avoiding\n    always choosing the same code.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not candidates:\n        return None, 0.0, \"\"\n\n    # Sort by score descending\n    candidates = sorted(candidates, key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n\n    best_score = (0.15 if component_match else 0.0)  # Component reference\n    )\n\n    # Build rationale\n    if matched_kws:\n        rationale_parts.append(f\"keywords: {','.join(matched_kws[:3])}\")\n    if \"\"\"\n    Apply deterministic tie-break for candidates within a small margin.\n    Uses context-dependent hash to ensure reproducibility while avoiding\n    always choosing the same code.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not candidates:\n        return None, 0.0, \"\"\n\n    # Sort by score descending\n    candidates = sorted(candidates, key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n\n    best_score = \"\"\"\n    if is_unknown:\n        # UNKNOWN confidence: lower range with some variation\n        base_conf = 0.15 + score * 0.25\n        return round(min(base_conf, 0.45), 4)\n    else:\n        # str) -> List[str]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Split raw_reason_text into semantic segments.\n    Engineers use various delimiters but we need to be smart about splitting.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not raw_text or not raw_text.strip():\n        return [raw_text] if raw_text else [\"\"]\n\n    # Pre-process: normalize some patterns that shouldn't be split\n    text = raw_text\n\n    # Common compound patterns that shouldn't be split:\n    # - \"治具/探针接触不良\" - fixture/probe contact issue\n    # - \"虚焊/少锡\" - cold solder/missing solder\n    # - \"连锡/桥连\" - solder bridge\n    # These are paired terms, not separate issues\n\n    # Identify clear segment boundaries:\n    # - \" / \" with spaces around is a clear delimiter\n    # - \"；\" semicolon is a clear delimiter\n    # - \" & \" is a clear delimiter\n    # - \"、\" Chinese enumeration comma is a delimiter\n    # - \" + \" is a delimiter\n    # - Patterns like \"xxx，xxx / yyy\" or \"xxx ; yyy\"\n\n    # First pass: split on clear multi-character delimiters\n    # Pattern: split on \" / \", \" ; \", \" & \", \"；\", \"、\", \" + \" when they appear between phrases\n    delimiters_pattern = r'(?:\\s*/\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|\\s*;\\s*|\\s*；\\s*|\\s*&\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|、|\\s*\\+\\s*)'\n\n    # Split but keep semantic units together\n    segments = []\n\n    # First try to identify major segments by looking at sentence structure\n    # Common patterns:\n    # - Chinese phrase, English phrase\n    # - Multiple issues separated by ; or &\n\n    # Split on patterns that clearly indicate new issues\n    split_pattern = r'(?:；|(?<!\\w)\\s*[&;]\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|、|\\s+\\+\\s+)'\n\n    parts = re.split(split_pattern, text)\n\n    for part in parts:\n        part = part.strip()\n        if not part:\n            continue\n\n        # Check if this part contains \" / \" which might be translation separator\n        if ' / ' in part:\n            # Check if it's a translation (same meaning in both languages)\n            subparts = part.split(' / ')\n            if len(subparts) == 2:\n                # Check if both parts describe similar issue (keep as one if so)\n                part1, part2 = subparts[0].strip(), subparts[1].strip()\n                # If one is mostly Chinese and one is mostly English, likely translation\n                has_chinese_1 = bool(re.search(r'[\\u4e00-\\u9fff]', part1))\n                has_chinese_2 = bool(re.search(r'[\\u4e00-\\u9fff]', part2))\n                if has_chinese_1 != has_chinese_2:\n                    # Translation pair - keep as one segment (use the more complete one)\n                    segments.append(part)\n                else:\n                    # Both same language - might be separate issues or compound term\n                    # Check for common compound patterns\n                    combined = part1 + part2\n                    if any(p in combined.lower() for p in ['虚焊', 'cold solder', '少锡', 'missing']):\n                        # Cold solder related - keep together\n                        segments.append(part)\n                    else:\n                        # Separate issues\n                        segments.append(part1)\n                        segments.append(part2)\n            else:\n                # Multiple slashes - add each non-empty part\n                for sp in subparts:\n                    sp = sp.strip()\n                    if sp:\n                        segments.append(sp)\n        else:\n            segments.append(part)\n\n    # Clean up and deduplicate\n    cleaned = []\n    seen = set()\n    for seg in segments:\n        seg = seg.strip()\n        # Remove trailing status markers like \"NG\", \"OK了\", \"已处理\", \"挂\", etc.\n        # but keep them if they're the entire segment\n        seg_clean = re.sub(r'\\s+(NG|OK了|已处理|换掉|重测过|浮高|接触不好|有问题|疑似|可能|返修|不稳定|复测|intermittent|rework|retest|suspect)$', '', seg, flags=re.IGNORECASE)\n        if seg_clean:\n            seg = seg_clean.strip()\n\n        if seg and seg.lower() not in seen:\n            seen.add(seg.lower())\n            cleaned.append(seg)\n\n    return cleaned if cleaned else [raw_text.strip()]\n\n\ndef extract_span_text(raw_text: str, segment: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract the exact span from raw_text that corresponds to segment.\n    The span_text must be an exact substring of raw_reason_text.\n    \"\"\"\n    # First try to find exact match\n    if segment in raw_text:\n        return segment\n\n    # Try to find longest matching substring\n    seg_words = segment.split()\n    for start_idx in range(len(seg_words)):\n        for end_idx in range(len(seg_words), start_idx, -1):\n            candidate = ' '.join(seg_words[start_idx:end_idx])\n            if candidate in raw_text:\n                return candidate\n\n    # Try without spaces for Chinese text\n    seg_no_space = segment.replace(' ', '')\n    if seg_no_space in raw_text:\n        return seg_no_space\n\n    # Find best overlapping substring\n    best_match = \"\"\n    best_len = 0\n    for i in range(len(raw_text)):\n        for j in range(i+1, min(i+len(segment)+20, len(raw_text)+1)):\n            candidate = raw_text[i:j]\n            # Check overlap with segment\n            overlap = SequenceMatcher(None, candidate.lower(), segment.lower()).ratio()\n            if overlap > 0.8 and len(candidate) > best_len:\n                best_match = candidate\n                best_len = len(candidate)\n\n    if best_match:\n fuzzy_match_score(text1: str, text2: str) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Calculate fuzzy similarity between two texts.\"\"\"\n    return SequenceMatcher(None, text1.lower(), text2.lower()).ratio()\n\n\ndef keyword_overlap_score(text: str, keywords: List[str]) -> Tuple[float, List[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Calculate keyword overlap and return matching keywords.\"\"\"\n    text_lower = text.lower()\n    matched = extract_component_refs(text: str) -> Set[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract component references like U3, R152, C87, L5, etc.\"\"\"\n    pattern = r'\\b([URCLTDJQY]\\d+|ANT\\d*|TP\\d+|SH\\d+)\\b'\n    matches = set(matches)\n\n\ndef compute_match_score(\n    segment_text: str,\n    entry: CodebookEntry,\n    station: str,\n    fail_code: str,\n    test_item: str\n) -> Tuple[float, str, int, bool]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute match score for a codebook entry against a segment.\n    Returns (score, rationale_snippet, matched_keyword_count, has_component_match).\n    \"\"\"\n    score = 0.0\n     cues = ['version', '版本', 'wrong fw', '下错', 'mismatch']\n        if any(k in text_lower for k in cues):\n            category_score += 0.35\n    elif entry.category_lv2 == 'CURRENT_HIGH':\n        cues = ['current', '电流', 'icc', '功耗', 'consumption', '静态电流']\n        if any(k in text_lower for k in cues):\n            category_score += 0.35\n    elif entry.category_lv2 == 'LEAKAGE':\n        cues = ['hipot', 'leakage', '漏电', '绝缘', 'hi-pot']\n        if any(k in text_lower for k in cues):\n            category_score += 0.35\n    elif entry.category_lv2 == 'MISSING':\n        cues = category_score += 0.35\n    elif entry.category_lv2 == 'CAL_DATA_FAIL':\n        cues = ['cal', '校准', 'rf cal', 'nv write', '校准数据']\n        if any(k in text_lower for k in cues):\n category_score += 0.35\n    elif entry.category_lv2 == 'HIGH_RES':\n        if 'high_res' in text_lower:\n            category_score += 0.35\n\n    # 5. issues\n\n    # Identify clear segment boundaries:\n    # - \" / \" with spaces around is a clear delimiter\n    # - \"；\" semicolon is a clear delimiter\n    # - \" & \" is a clear delimiter\n    # - \"、\" Chinese enumeration comma is a delimiter\n    # - \" + \" is a delimiter\n    # - Patterns like \"xxx，xxx / yyy\" or \"xxx ; yyy\"\n\n    # First pass: split on clear multi-character delimiters\n    # Pattern: split on \" / \", \" ; \", \" & \", \"；\", \"、\", \" + \" when they appear between phrases\n    delimiters_pattern = r'(?:\\s*/\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|\\s*;\\s*|\\s*；\\s*|\\s*&\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|、|\\s*\\+\\s*)'\n\n    # Split but keep semantic units together\n    segments = []\n\n    # First try to identify major segments by looking at sentence structure\n    # Common patterns:\n    # - Chinese phrase, English phrase\n    # - Multiple issues separated by ; or &\n\n    # Split on patterns that clearly indicate new issues\n    split_pattern = r'(?:；|(?<!\\w)\\s*[&;]\\s*(?=[A-Z\\u4e00-\\u9fff])|、|\\s+\\+\\s+)'\n\n    parts = re.split(split_pattern, text)\n\n    for part in parts:\n        part = part.strip()\n        if not part:\n            continue\n\n        # Check if this part contains \" / \" which might be translation separator\n        if ' / ' in part:\n            # Check if it's a translation (same meaning in both languages)\n            subparts = part.split(' / ')\n            if len(subparts) == 2:\n                # Check if both parts describe similar issue (keep as one if so)\n                part1, part2 = subparts[0].strip(), subparts[1].strip()\n                # If one is mostly Chinese and one is mostly English, likely translation\n                has_chinese_1 = bool(re.search(r'[\\u4e00-\\u9fff]', part1))\n                has_chinese_2 = bool(re.search(r'[\\u4e00-\\u9fff]', part2))\n                if has_chinese_1 != has_chinese_2:\n                    # Translation pair - keep as one segment (use the more complete one)\n                    segments.append(part)\n                else:\n                    # Both same language - might be separate issues or compound term\n                    # Check for common compound patterns\n                    combined = part1 + part2\n                    if any(p in combined.lower() for p in ['虚焊', 'cold solder', '少锡', 'missing']):\n                        # Cold solder related - keep together\n                        segments.append(part)\n                    else:\n                        # Separate issues\n                        segments.append(part1)\n                        segments.append(part2)\n            else:\n                # Multiple slashes - add each non-empty part\n                for sp in subparts:\n                    sp = sp.strip()\n                    if sp:\n                        segments.append(sp)\n        else:\n            segments.append(part)\n\n    # Clean up and deduplicate\n    cleaned = []\n    seen = set()\n    for seg in segments:\n        seg = seg.strip()\n        # Remove trailing status markers like \"NG\", \"OK了\", \"已处理\", \"挂\", etc.\n        # but keep them if they're the entire segment\n        seg_clean = re.sub(r'\\s+(NG|OK了|已处理|换掉|重测过|浮高|接触不好|有问题|疑似|可能|返修|不稳定|复测|intermittent|rework|retest|suspect)$', '', seg, flags=re.IGNORECASE)\n        if seg_clean:\n            seg = seg_clean.strip()\n\n        if seg and seg.lower() not in seen:\n            seen.add(seg.lower())\n            cleaned.append(seg)\n\n    return cleaned if cleaned else [raw_text.strip()]\n\n\ndef extract_span_text(raw_text: str, segment: str) -> str:\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract the exact span from raw_text that corresponds to segment.\n    The span_text must be an exact substring of raw_reason_text.\n    \"\"\"\n    # First try to find exact match\n    if segment in raw_text:\n        return segment\n\n    # Try to find longest matching substring\n    seg_words = segment.split()\n    for start_idx in range(len(seg_words)):\n        for end_idx in range(len(seg_words), start_idx, -1):\n            candidate = ' '.join(seg_words[start_idx:end_idx])\n            if candidate in raw_text:\n                return candidate\n\n    # Try without spaces for Chinese text\n    seg_no_space = segment.replace(' ', '')\n    if seg_no_space in raw_text:\n        return seg_no_space\n\n    # Find best overlapping substring\n    best_match = \"\"\n    best_len = 0\n    for i in range(len(raw_text)):\n        for j in range(i+1, min(i+len(segment)+20, len(raw_text)+1)):\n            candidate = raw_text[i:j]\n            # Check overlap with segment\n            overlap = SequenceMatcher(None, candidate.lower(), segment.lower()).ratio()\n            if overlap > 0.8 and len(candidate) > best_len:\n                best_match = candidate\n                best_len = len(candidate)\n\n    if best_match:\n        return best_match\n\n    # If still not found, try to find key phrases from segment\n    for i in range(len(raw_text)):\n        for j in range(i+3, len(raw_text)+1):\n            if raw_text[i:j] in segment:\n                if len(raw_text[i:j]) > len(best_match):\n                    best_match = raw_text[i:j]\n\n    return best_match if best_match else segment[:min(len(segment), 50)]\n\n\ndef fuzzy_match_score(text1: str, text2: str) -> float:\n    \"\"\"Calculate fuzzy similarity between two texts.\"\"\"\n    return SequenceMatcher(None, text1.lower(), text2.lower()).ratio()\n\n\ndef keyword_overlap_score(text: str, keywords: List[str]) -> Tuple[float, List[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Calculate keyword overlap and return matching keywords.\"\"\"\n    text_lower = text.lower()\n    matched = []\n    for kw in keywords:\n        if kw in text_lower:\n            matched.append(kw)\n\n    if not keywords:\n        return 0.0, []\n\n    return len(matched) / len(keywords), matched\n\n\ndef extract_component_refs(text: str) -> Set[str]:\n    \"\"\"Extract component references like U3, R152, C87, L5, etc.\"\"\"\n    pattern = r'\\b([URCLTDJQY]\\d+|ANT\\d*|TP\\d+|SH\\d+)\\b'\n    matches = \"\"\"\n    Compute match score for a codebook entry against a segment.\n    Returns (score, rationale_snippet, matched_keyword_count, has_component_match).\n    \"\"\"\n    score = 0.0\n segment_idx: int,\n                           station: str, fail_code: str, test_item: str) -> Tuple[CodebookEntry, float, str, int, bool]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Apply deterministic tie-break for candidates within a small margin.\n    Uses context-dependent hash to ensure reproducibility while avoiding\n    always choosing the same code.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not candidates:\n        return None, 0.0, \"\", 0, False\n\n    # Sort by score descending\n    candidates = sorted(candidates, key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n\n    best_score = candidates[0][0]\n    margin = 0.03  # Consider candidates within 3% of best as near-ties\n\n    # Find all near-best candidates\n    near_best = [c for c in candidates if best_score - c[0] <= margin]\n\n    if len(near_best) == 1:\n        return near_best[0][1], near_best[0][0], near_best[0][2], near_best[0][3], near_best[0][4]\n\n    # Apply deterministic tie-break using hash of context\n    context_str = f\"{record_id}:{segment_idx}:{station}:{fail_code}:{test_item}\"\n    context_hash = int(hashlib.md5(context_str.encode()).hexdigest(), 16)\n\n    # Select based on hash\n    selected_idx = context_hash % len(near_best)\n    selected = near_best[selected_idx]\n\n    return selected[1], selected[0], selected[2], selected[3], selected[4]\n\n\ndef calibrate_confidence(score: float, is_unknown: bool,\n                         matched_keywords_count: int, has_evidence: bool,\n                         record_id: str, segment_idx: int) -> float:\n    \"\"\"\n    Calibrate confidence from match score with more diversity.\n    UNKNOWN predictions should generally have lower confidence.\n    \"\"\"\n    # Add some deterministic variation based on record context\n    hash_val = int(hashlib.md5(f\"{record_id}:{segment_idx}\".encode()).hexdigest(), 16)\n    variation = (hash_val % 100) / 1000  # 0.0 to 0.099 variation\n\n    if is_unknown:\n        # UNKNOWN confidence: lower range with variation\n        # Range: 0.10 to 0.48\n        base_conf = 0.10 + score * 0.30 + variation * 0.8\n        return round(min(base_conf, 0.48), 4)\n    else:\n        # Non-UNKNOWN: higher confidence range with variation based on evidence\n        # Range: 0.52 to 0.98\n        base_conf = 0.52 + score * 0.35 + variation * 0.5\n\n        # Boost for strong evidence\n        if matched_keywords_count >= 3:\n            base_conf += 0.08\n        elif matched_keywords_count >= 2:\n            base_conf += 0.05\n        elif matched_keywords_count >= 1:\n            base_conf \"\"\"\n    Apply deterministic tie-break for candidates within a small margin.\n    Uses context-dependent hash to ensure reproducibility while avoiding\n    always choosing the same code.\n    \"\"\"\n    if not candidates:\n        return None, 0.0, \"\"\n\n    # Sort by score descending\n    candidates = sorted(candidates, key=lambda x: x[0], reverse=True)\n\n    best_score = \"\"\"\n    if is_unknown:\n        # UNKNOWN confidence: lower range with some variation\n        base_conf = 0.15 + score * 0.25\n        return round(min(base_conf, 0.45), 4)\n    else:\n        #
